```
PATH: crates/ars-components/src/date_time/date_picker/mod.rs:752

**<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Clear stale controlled focus intents after vetoed opens**

When `open` is controlled, a focus-driven open request stores `Some(OpenFocus::None)` here and waits for `SyncProps` to consume it. If the parent vetoes that request by leaving `open: Some(false)`, `SyncProps` never runs, so the marker remains; a later programmatic `open` prop change then treats the stale `None` as the originating focus intent and suppresses the default `FocusCalendar` effect. In controlled pickers that do not honor every focus-open request, the next real open can leave focus in the wrong place.

Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.

=== THREAD PRRT_kwDORp4enM6F8jrV ===
PATH: crates/ars-components/src/date_time/date_picker/mod.rs:424
PARENT_DBID: 3330370979
AUTHOR: chatgpt-codex-connector

**<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Preserve ICU field order for native-digit locales**

For locales whose ICU short date uses native digits, `date_order(locale)` can fall back to the heuristic instead of the real CLDR order, but this default format trusts it directly. For example, with the default ICU backend `fa-IR` formats a probe date with the year first (`۱۳۸۵/۹/۱`), while `date_order(&fa_IR)` returns `DayMonthYear` because the parser does not recognize those digits; the DatePicker then defaults to `dd/MM/yyyy`, so Persian users type and see the wrong field order even though ICU data is available.

Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.

=== THREAD PRRT_kwDORp4enM6F8jrW ===
PATH: crates/ars-components/src/date_time/date_picker/mod.rs:1031

AUTHOR: chatgpt-codex-connector

**<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Keep closed pickers closed on stale selections**

When `close_on_select` is false, any `SelectDate` event received while the uncontrolled picker is already closed transitions it to `Open`. A queued/stale calendar selection (or a scripted event) should not reopen the popover; the “stay open” case should preserve the current state, otherwise a date click that races with an outside-close/focus-out can bring the calendar back after the user closed it.

Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.
```

```
399	/// One of the three ordered fields in a date format pattern.
400	#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
401	enum DateField {
402	    Year,
403	    Month,
404	    Day,
405	}
406	
407	/// Returns the locale-appropriate default date format pattern.
408	///
409	/// Field order comes from [`ars_i18n::date_order`], which uses real locale data
410	/// — CLDR via ICU4X or the browser `Intl` API where a backend is compiled in,
411	/// and a `(language, region)` heuristic otherwise — so every locale gets the
412	/// correct month/day/year order (the sibling [`date_field`](super::date_field)
413	/// uses the same source). The field separator stays a small locale heuristic
414	/// (`.` for German and Korean, `/` elsewhere), mirroring `date_field`'s literal.
415	fn default_format_for_locale(locale: &Locale) -> String {
416	    let separator = match (locale.language(), locale.region()) {
417	        ("de", Some("DE")) | ("ko", Some("KR")) => '.',
418	        _ => '/',
419	    };
420	
421	    let order: [&str; 3] = match date_order(locale) {
422	        DateOrder::MonthDayYear => ["MM", "dd", "yyyy"],
423	        DateOrder::DayMonthYear => ["dd", "MM", "yyyy"],
424	        DateOrder::YearMonthDay => ["yyyy", "MM", "dd"],
425	    };
426	
427	    format!("{}{separator}{}{separator}{}", order[0], order[1], order[2])
428	}
429	
430	/// Parses a format pattern into its field separator and ordered fields.
431	///
432	/// The separator is the first non-alphabetic character (defaulting to `'/'`);
433	/// each token's leading letter selects the field (`y`→year, `M`→month,
434	/// `d`→day). Patterns that do not split into three tokens fall back to
435	/// month/day/year.
436	fn parse_format(format: &str) -> (char, [DateField; 3]) {
437	    let sep = format
438	        .chars()
439	        .find(|c| !c.is_ascii_alphabetic())
440	        .unwrap_or('/');
441	
442	    let mut order = [DateField::Month, DateField::Day, DateField::Year];
443	
444	    let tokens = format.split(sep).collect::<Vec<_>>();
445	
446	    if tokens.len() == 3 {
447	        for (index, token) in tokens.iter().enumerate() {
448	            order[index] = match token.chars().next() {
449	                Some('y' | 'Y') => DateField::Year,
450	                Some('d' | 'D') => DateField::Day,
451	                _ => DateField::Month,
452	            };
453	        }
454	    }
455	
456	    (sep, order)
457	}
458	
459	/// Formats a [`CalendarDate`] according to the given pattern.
460	///
461	/// Month and day are zero-padded to two digits, year to four, in the field
462	/// order described by `format`.
463	fn format_date(date: &CalendarDate, format: &str) -> String {
464	    let (sep, order) = parse_format(format);
465	
466	    let mut out = String::new();
467	
468	    for (index, field) in order.iter().enumerate() {
469	        if index > 0 {
470	            out.push(sep);
471	        }
472	
473	        match field {
474	            DateField::Year => {
475	                let _ = write!(out, "{:04}", date.year());
476	            }
477	
478	            DateField::Month => {
479	                let _ = write!(out, "{:02}", date.month());
480	            }
481	
482	            DateField::Day => {
483	                let _ = write!(out, "{:02}", date.day());
484	            }
485	        }
486	    }
487	
488	    out
489	}
490

495	fn parse_date(text: &str, format: &str) -> Option<CalendarDate> {
496	    let (sep, order) = parse_format(format);
497	
498	    let tokens = text.split(sep).collect::<Vec<_>>();
```

```
705	impl OpenFocus {
706	    fn effect(self) -> Option<PendingEffect<Machine>> {
707	        match self {
708	            OpenFocus::Calendar => Some(focus_calendar_effect()),
709	            OpenFocus::Trigger => Some(restore_focus_to_trigger_effect()),
710	            OpenFocus::Input => Some(restore_focus_to_input_effect()),

715	
716	/// Builds the plan for a user-driven request to open or close the popover.
717	///
718	/// When `open` is **controlled** (`props.open.is_some()`), the [`State`] is not
719	/// mutated: the request is recorded in [`Context::requested_open`] (and the
720	/// `focus` intent in `requested_focus`) and signalled via [`Effect::OpenChange`]
721	/// so the parent reconciles by updating the `open` prop — the focus effect then
722	/// fires from `SyncProps` once the state actually lands. When **uncontrolled**,
723	/// the `State` transitions and the `focus` effect fires immediately.
724	///
725	/// Returns `None` when the popover is already in the requested open state.
726	fn open_request(
727	    state: &State,
728	    props: &Props,
729	    target_open: bool,
730	    focus: OpenFocus,
731	) -> Option<TransitionPlan<Machine>> {
732	    if (*state == State::Open) == target_open {
733	        return None;
734	    }
735	
736	    let controlled = props.open.is_some();
737	    let mut plan = if controlled {
738	        TransitionPlan::new()
739	    } else {
740	        TransitionPlan::to(if target_open {
741	            State::Open
742	        } else {
743	            State::Closed
744	        })
745	    };
746	
747	    plan = plan
748	        .apply(move |ctx: &mut Context| {
749	            ctx.requested_open = target_open;
750	            if controlled {
751	                // Defer the focus move to `SyncProps`, which lands the state.
752	                ctx.requested_focus = Some(focus);
753	            }
754	        })
755	        .with_effect(open_change_effect());
756	
757	    if !controlled && let Some(focus) = focus.effect() {
758	        plan = plan.with_effect(focus);
759	    }
760	
761	    Some(plan)
762	}
763	
764	/// Re-applies the controlled value and cached prop fields onto `ctx`.
765	///
766	/// The controlled `value` flows through its [`Bindable`] via
767	/// [`Bindable::sync_controlled`] (`Some(_)` stays controlled, `None` reveals the
768	/// uncontrolled internal value), then the cached scalar fields are refreshed.
769	/// Open/closed is owned by [`State`] (the [`Event::SyncProps`] transition derives
770	/// the target state from `props.open`), so there is no `open` field to refresh
771	/// here.
772	///
773	/// The input text is re-synced **only when the displayed value or format
774	/// actually changed** — an unrelated prop change (e.g. `invalid`, `description`,
775	/// `disabled`) must not clobber a partial/invalid date the user is mid-way
776	/// through typing.
777	fn sync_props_into_ctx(ctx: &mut Context, props: &Props) {
778	    let previous_display = ctx.formatted_value();
779	
780	    ctx.value.sync_controlled(props.value.clone());
781	
782	    ctx.min = props.min.clone();
783	    ctx.max = props.max.clone();
784	    ctx.today = props.today.clone();
785	    ctx.disabled = props.disabled;
786	    ctx.readonly = props.readonly;
787	    ctx.open_on_click = props.open_on_click;
788	    ctx.name = props.name.clone();
789	    ctx.required = props.required;
790	    ctx.is_rtl = props.is_rtl;
791	    ctx.format = props
792	        .format
793	        .clone()
794	        .unwrap_or_else(|| default_format_for_locale(&ctx.locale));
795	
796	    let next_display = ctx.formatted_value();
797	    if next_display != previous_display {
798	        ctx.input_text = next_display;
799	    }
800	}
801
```

```
906	
907	    fn transition(
908	        state: &Self::State,
909	        event: &Self::Event,
910	        ctx: &Self::Context,
911	        props: &Self::Props,
912	    ) -> Option<TransitionPlan<Self>> {
913	        // `SyncProps` flows through even when disabled so parent-driven changes
914	        // that take the picker *out of* the disabled state can land.
915	        if let Event::SyncProps(new_props) = event {
916	            let new_props = new_props.as_ref().clone();
917	
918	            let was_open = *state == State::Open;
919	
920	            let target = new_props
921	                .open
922	                .map(|open| if open { State::Open } else { State::Closed });
923	
924	            let opening = !was_open && target.as_ref() == Some(&State::Open);
925	            let closing = was_open && target.as_ref() == Some(&State::Closed);
926	
927	            // The focus intent recorded by the user event that prompted this
928	            // reconciliation (read before the apply closure consumes it). When a
929	            // controlled change is purely programmatic (no preceding user event),
930	            // it is `None` and the per-direction default applies.
931	            let pending_focus = ctx.requested_focus;
932	
933	            let mut plan = if let Some(next) = target {
934	                TransitionPlan::to(next)
935	            } else {
936	                TransitionPlan::new()
937	            };
938	
939	            plan = plan.apply(move |ctx: &mut Context| {
940	                if let Some(open) = new_props.open {
941	                    ctx.requested_open = open;
942	                }
943	                // Consume the pending focus intent.
944	                ctx.requested_focus = None;
945	                sync_props_into_ctx(ctx, &new_props);
946	            });
947	
948	            // No `OpenChange` here: a controlled `open` prop change is the
949	            // parent's own doing, so re-notifying would double-fire (and loop
950	            // against a user request that prompted the prop change). Focus
951	            // reproduces the originating user intent (or the per-direction
952	            // default for a programmatic change).
953	            if opening && let Some(focus) = pending_focus.unwrap_or(OpenFocus::Calendar).effect() {
954	                plan = plan.with_effect(focus);
955	            } else if closing
956	                && let Some(focus) = pending_focus.unwrap_or(OpenFocus::Trigger).effect()
957	            {
958	                plan = plan.with_effect(focus);
959	            }
960	
961	            return Some(plan);
962	        }
963	
964	        if ctx.disabled {
965	            return None;
966	        }
967	
968	        match event {
969	            // Trigger / `ArrowDown` open: when uncontrolled this opens and moves
970	            // focus into the calendar; when controlled it only requests the open
971	            // (see `open_request`). `readonly` blocks opening entirely.
972	            Event::Open => {
973	                if ctx.readonly {
974	                    return None;
975	                }
976	                open_request(state, props, true, OpenFocus::Calendar)
977	            }
978	
979	            Event::Close => open_request(state, props, false, OpenFocus::Trigger),
980	
981	            Event::Toggle => match state {
982	                State::Closed => Self::transition(state, &Event::Open, ctx, props),
983	                State::Open => Self::transition(state, &Event::Close, ctx, props),
984	            },
985	
986	            Event::SelectDate { date } => {
987	                if ctx.readonly {
988	                    return None;
989	                }
990	
991	                // Defense-in-depth: reject a selection the picker disallows. The
992	                // embedded calendar should never offer an out-of-range or
993	                // unavailable date, but a scripted/stale/buggy `SelectDate` must
994	                // not be able to commit a value the component forbids (the typed
995	                // path enforces the same constraints).
996	                let in_range = ctx
997	                    .min
998	                    .as_ref()
999	                    .is_none_or(|m| date.compare(m) != Ordering::Less)
1000	                    && ctx
1001	                        .max
1002	                        .as_ref()
1003	                        .is_none_or(|m| date.compare(m) != Ordering::Greater);
1004	                let available = props
1005	                    .is_date_unavailable
1006	                    .as_ref()
1007	                    .is_none_or(|predicate| !predicate(date));
1008	                if !(in_range && available) {
1009	                    return None;
1010	                }
1011	
1012	                let date = date.clone();
1013	                let should_close = props.close_on_select;
1014	                let open_controlled = props.open.is_some();
1015	                // Only commit the close when open is uncontrolled; a controlled
1016	                // picker requests the close and lets the parent reconcile.
1017	                let commit_close = should_close && !open_controlled;
1018	                // Suppress a no-op value-change notification when the selected
1019	                // date already matches the committed value.
1020	                let value_changes = ctx.value.get().as_ref() != Some(&date);
1021	
1022	                // When `open` is controlled, never mutate `State` here — the
1023	                // parent owns it (a stale/scripted selection must not locally
1024	                // reopen or move the picker). Otherwise close on select (or stay
1025	                // open when `close_on_select` is false).
1026	                let next_state = if open_controlled {
```

```
1100	                                    .is_none_or(|m| date.compare(m) != Ordering::Greater);
```

```
1866	// Codex review #697 (pass 5, P5-4) — focus intent through controlled sync
1867	// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
1868	//
1869	// For controlled `open`, the state change lands in `SyncProps` (the parent
1870	// echoes the prop), and the focus effect must reproduce the *originating* user
1871	// intent rather than always focusing the calendar on open / the trigger on
1872	// close.
1873	
1874	/// Drives a controlled-open round trip: the user event is sent (recording the
1875	/// request without changing `State`), then the parent echoes `open` via
1876	/// `set_props`, and we return the effects emitted by that reconciliation.
1877	fn controlled_reconcile(initial_open: bool, user_event: Event, echoed_open: bool) -> Vec<Effect> {
1878	    let mut svc = service_with(
1879	        Props {
1880	            open: Some(initial_open),
1881	            default_value: Some(date(2024, 3, 15)),
1882	            ..props()
1883	        },
1884	        en_us(),
1885	    );
1886	    drop(svc.send(user_event));
1887	    // State is unchanged by the user event under controlled open.
1888	    assert_eq!(*svc.state() == State::Open, initial_open);
1889	    effects(svc.set_props(Props {
1890	        open: Some(echoed_open),
1891	        default_value: Some(date(2024, 3, 15)),
1892	        ..props()
1893	    }))
1894	}
1895	
1896	#[test]
1897	fn controlled_focus_in_open_keeps_input_focus_through_sync() {
1898	    // Input-focus open then parent echoes open=true: focus must NOT move into the
1899	    // calendar (no FocusCalendar), unlike a trigger open.
1900	    let emitted = controlled_reconcile(false, Event::FocusIn, true);
1901	    assert!(!emitted.contains(&Effect::FocusCalendar));
1902	    assert!(!emitted.contains(&Effect::RestoreFocusToTrigger));
1903	}
1904	
1905	#[test]
1906	fn controlled_trigger_open_focuses_calendar_through_sync() {
1907	    // Trigger open then echo: focus moves into the calendar.
1908	    let emitted = controlled_reconcile(false, Event::Open, true);
1909	    assert!(emitted.contains(&Effect::FocusCalendar));
1910	}
1911	
1912	#[test]
1913	fn controlled_close_restores_trigger_through_sync() {
1914	    let emitted = controlled_reconcile(true, Event::Close, false);
1915	    assert!(emitted.contains(&Effect::RestoreFocusToTrigger));
1916	    assert!(!emitted.contains(&Effect::RestoreFocusToInput));
1917	}
1918	
1919	#[test]
1920	fn controlled_select_close_restores_input_through_sync() {
1921	    // SelectDate-close then echo close: focus restores to the INPUT, not trigger.
1922	    let emitted = controlled_reconcile(
1923	        true,
1924	        Event::SelectDate {
1925	            date: date(2024, 4, 20),
1926	        },
1927	        false,
1928	    );
1929	    assert!(emitted.contains(&Effect::RestoreFocusToInput));
1930	    assert!(!emitted.contains(&Effect::RestoreFocusToTrigger));
1931	}
1932	
1933	#[test]
1934	fn controlled_focus_out_close_steals_no_focus_through_sync() {
1935	    // FocusOut close then echo: no focus effect (focus already left the field).
1936	    let emitted = controlled_reconcile(true, Event::FocusOut, false);
1937	    assert!(!emitted.contains(&Effect::RestoreFocusToTrigger));
1938	    assert!(!emitted.contains(&Effect::RestoreFocusToInput));
1939	    assert!(!emitted.contains(&Effect::FocusCalendar));
1940	}

1942	#[test]

1944	    // No preceding user event: a programmatic controlled open uses the

1956	        ..props()

1958	
1959	    assert!(emitted.contains(&Effect::FocusCalendar));
1960	}
```

```
1254	/// Returns the locale's preferred order of the year, month, and day fields in a
1255	/// numeric date.
1256	///
1257	/// Uses real locale data when a backend is compiled in — CLDR via ICU4X (the
1258	/// `icu4x` feature) or the browser `Intl` API (`web-intl` on wasm) — and falls
1259	/// back to a `(language, region)` heuristic otherwise. This is the reliable
1260	/// source for date field ordering: components should call it instead of
1261	/// hand-maintaining per-locale tables.
1262	#[must_use]
1263	pub fn date_order(locale: &Locale) -> DateOrder {
1264	    #[cfg(feature = "icu4x")]
1265	    if let Some(order) = icu_date_order(locale) {
1266	        return order;
1267	    }
1268	
1269	    #[cfg(all(feature = "web-intl", target_arch = "wasm32", not(feature = "icu4x")))]
1270	    if let Some(order) = js_date_order(locale) {
1271	        return order;
1272	    }
1273	
1274	    fallback_date_order(locale)
1275	}
1276	
1277	#[cfg(feature = "icu4x")]
1278	fn icu_date_order(locale: &Locale) -> Option<DateOrder> {
1279	    let probe = CalendarDate::new_gregorian(2006, 11, 22).ok()?;
1280	
1281	    let formatter = build_icu_date_formatter(locale, FormatLength::Short);
1282	
1283	    let formatted = format_icu_date(locale, FormatLength::Short, &formatter, &probe);
1284	
1285	    numeric_field_order_from_formatted(&formatted)
1286	}

1289	fn js_date_order(locale: &Locale) -> Option<DateOrder> {
1290	    let probe = CalendarDate::new_gregorian(2006, 11, 22).ok()?;
1291	
1292	    let formatter = build_js_date_formatter(locale, FormatLength::Short);
1293

1299	fn fallback_date_order(locale: &Locale) -> DateOrder {
1300	    if matches!(locale.language(), "ja" | "zh" | "ko") {
1301	        return DateOrder::YearMonthDay;
1302	    }
1303	
1304	    match locale.region() {
1305	        Some("US" | "FM" | "PW") => DateOrder::MonthDayYear,
1306	
1307	        Some("CA" | "CN" | "HU" | "JP" | "KR" | "LT" | "MN" | "TW") => DateOrder::YearMonthDay,
1308	
1309	        _ => DateOrder::DayMonthYear,
1310	    }
1311	}
1312	
1313	#[cfg(any(
1314	    feature = "icu4x",
1315	    all(feature = "web-intl", target_arch = "wasm32", not(feature = "icu4x"))
1316	))]
1317	fn numeric_field_order_from_formatted(formatted: &str) -> Option<DateOrder> {
1318	    #[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
1319	    enum DateComponent {
1320	        Month,
1321	        Day,
1322	        Year,
1323	    }
1324	
1325	    let mut numeric_runs = Vec::new();
1326	
1327	    let mut current: Option<u32> = None;
1328	
1329	    for ch in formatted.chars() {
1330	        if let Some(digit) = ch.to_digit(10) {
1331	            current = Some(current.unwrap_or(0).checked_mul(10)?.checked_add(digit)?);
1332	        } else if let Some(value) = current.take() {
1333	            numeric_runs.push(value);
1334	        }
1335	    }
1336	
1337	    if let Some(value) = current {
1338	        numeric_runs.push(value);
1339	    }
1340	
1341	    let components: Vec<_> = numeric_runs
1342	        .into_iter()
1343	        .filter_map(|value| match value {
1344	            11 => Some(DateComponent::Month),
1345	            22 => Some(DateComponent::Day),
1346	            6 | 2006 => Some(DateComponent::Year),
1347	            _ => None,
1348	        })
1349	        .collect();
1350	
1351	    match components.as_slice() {
1352	        [
1353	            DateComponent::Month,
1354	            DateComponent::Day,
1355	            DateComponent::Year,
1356	        ] => Some(DateOrder::MonthDayYear),
1357	
1358	        [
1359	            DateComponent::Day,
1360	            DateComponent::Month,
1361	            DateComponent::Year,
1362	        ] => Some(DateOrder::DayMonthYear),
1363	
1364	        [
1365	            DateComponent::Year,
1366	            DateComponent::Month,
1367	            DateComponent::Day,
1368	        ] => Some(DateOrder::YearMonthDay),
1369	
1370	        _ => None,
1371	    }
1372	}
1373
```

```
545	/// Normalize non-Latin decimal digits into ASCII digits.
546	#[must_use]
547	pub fn normalize_digits(input: &str) -> String {
548	    input
549	        .chars()
550	        .map(|c| {
551	            if c.is_ascii_digit() {
552	                c
553	            } else if ('\u{0660}'..='\u{0669}').contains(&c) {
554	                char::from(b'0' + (c as u32 - 0x0660) as u8)
555	            } else if ('\u{06F0}'..='\u{06F9}').contains(&c) {
556	                char::from(b'0' + (c as u32 - 0x06F0) as u8)
557	            } else if ('\u{0966}'..='\u{096F}').contains(&c) {
558	                char::from(b'0' + (c as u32 - 0x0966) as u8)
559	            } else if ('\u{09E6}'..='\u{09EF}').contains(&c) {
560	                char::from(b'0' + (c as u32 - 0x09E6) as u8)
561	            } else {
562	                c
563	            }
564	        })
565	        .collect()
566	}
567	
568	/// Parse a locale-aware numeric string.
569	pub fn parse_locale_number(input: &str, locale: &Locale) -> Option<f64> {
570	    let transliterated = normalize_digits(input);
571	
572	    let (decimal_sep, group_sep) = decimal_and_group_separators(locale);
573	
574	    let filtered = filter_numeric_characters(&transliterated, decimal_sep, group_sep);
575	
576	    let chosen_decimal = choose_decimal_separator(&filtered, decimal_sep, group_sep);
577	
578	    let normalized = normalize_numeric_syntax(&filtered, chosen_decimal);
579	
580	    normalized.parse::<f64>().ok()
581	}
```

```
880	#[cfg(feature = "icu4x")]
881	fn format_icu_date(
882	    locale: &Locale,
883	    length: FormatLength,
884	    inner: &DateFormatterInner,
885	    date: &CalendarDate,
886	) -> String {
887	    let Ok(internal) = InternalCalendarDate::try_from(date) else {
888	        return fallback_format_date(locale, length, date);
889	    };
890	
891	    match inner {
892	        DateFormatterInner::Ymd(formatter) => formatter.format(&internal.inner).to_string(),
893	        DateFormatterInner::Ymde(formatter) => formatter.format(&internal.inner).to_string(),
894	    }
895	}

902	
903	    options.set_date_style(length.to_js_date_style());
904	    options.set_time_zone("UTC");

906	    JsDateTimeFormat::new(&locales, options.as_ref())
907	}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-c59961ed/crates/ars-i18n/src/locale.rs",
  "old_string": "    /// Returns the first day of week requested by the `u-fw-*` Unicode extension.",
  "new_string": "    /// Returns a clone of this locale with the Gregorian calendar forced via the\n    /// `u-ca-gregory` Unicode extension.\n    ///\n    /// Field-order detection ([`date_order`](crate::date_order)) formats a probe\n    /// date and reads back which field comes first. Locales that default to a\n    /// non-Gregorian calendar (for example `fa-IR`, which uses the Solar Hijri\n    /// calendar) would otherwise convert the probe and emit values that the order\n    /// parser cannot recognise; forcing the Gregorian calendar keeps the probe's\n    /// year/month/day values intact while preserving the locale's own field order.\n    #[must_use]\n    pub(crate) fn with_gregorian_calendar(&self) -> Self {\n        let mut icu = self.0.clone();\n        icu.extensions.unicode.keywords.set(\n            icu::locale::extensions::unicode::key!(\"ca\"),\n            icu::locale::extensions::unicode::value!(\"gregory\"),\n        );\n        Self(icu)\n    }\n\n    /// Returns the first day of week requested by the `u-fw-*` Unicode extension."
}
```

```json
  "old_string": "#[cfg(feature = \"icu4x\")]\nfn icu_date_order(locale: &Locale) -> Option<DateOrder> {\n    let probe = CalendarDate::new_gregorian(2006, 11, 22).ok()?;\n\n    let formatter = build_icu_date_formatter(locale, FormatLength::Short);\n\n    let formatted = format_icu_date(locale, FormatLength::Short, &formatter, &probe);\n\n    numeric_field_order_from_formatted(&formatted)\n}\n\n#[cfg(all(feature = \"web-intl\", target_arch = \"wasm32\", not(feature = \"icu4x\")))]\nfn js_date_order(locale: &Locale) -> Option<DateOrder> {\n    let probe = CalendarDate::new_gregorian(2006, 11, 22).ok()?;\n\n    let formatter = build_js_date_formatter(locale, FormatLength::Short);\n\n    let formatted = format_js_date(locale, FormatLength::Short, &formatter, &probe);\n\n    numeric_field_order_from_formatted(&formatted)\n}",
  "new_string": "#[cfg(feature = \"icu4x\")]\nfn icu_date_order(locale: &Locale) -> Option<DateOrder> {\n    let probe = CalendarDate::new_gregorian(2006, 11, 22).ok()?;\n\n    // Force the Gregorian calendar so non-Gregorian-default locales (e.g. the\n    // Solar Hijri default for `fa-IR`) keep the probe's recognisable\n    // year/month/day values instead of converting them away.\n    let locale = locale.with_gregorian_calendar();\n\n    let formatter = build_icu_date_formatter(&locale, FormatLength::Short);\n\n    let formatted = format_icu_date(&locale, FormatLength::Short, &formatter, &probe);\n\n    numeric_field_order_from_formatted(&formatted)\n}\n\n#[cfg(all(feature = \"web-intl\", target_arch = \"wasm32\", not(feature = \"icu4x\")))]\nfn js_date_order(locale: &Locale) -> Option<DateOrder> {\n    let probe = CalendarDate::new_gregorian(2006, 11, 22).ok()?;\n\n    // Force the Gregorian calendar (see `icu_date_order`): `Intl.DateTimeFormat`\n    // honours the `u-ca-gregory` extension carried in the BCP-47 tag.\n    let locale = locale.with_gregorian_calendar();\n\n    let formatter = build_js_date_formatter(&locale, FormatLength::Short);\n\n    let formatted = format_js_date(&locale, FormatLength::Short, &formatter, &probe);\n\n    numeric_field_order_from_formatted(&formatted)\n}"
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-c59961ed/crates/ars-components/src/date_time/date_picker/tests.rs",
  "old_string": "    assert_eq!(emitted, vec![Effect::FocusCalendar]);\n    assert_eq!(*svc.state(), State::Open);\n}\n\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// Snapshots — root\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────",
  "new_string": "    assert_eq!(emitted, vec![Effect::FocusCalendar]);\n    assert_eq!(*svc.state(), State::Open);\n}\n\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// Codex review #697 (pass 7) — stale-select reopen guard, vetoed-open focus\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n#[test]\nfn select_date_on_closed_uncontrolled_picker_stays_closed() {\n    // With `close_on_select == false`, a `SelectDate` that races in while an\n    // uncontrolled picker is already closed (a queued/scripted calendar\n    // selection, or a date click that lost to an outside-close/focus-out) must\n    // NOT reopen the popover. The \"stay open\" branch preserves the current\n    // state instead of forcing `Open`.\n    let mut svc = service_with(\n        Props {\n            close_on_select: false,\n            ..props()\n        },\n        en_us(),\n    );\n    assert_eq!(*svc.state(), State::Closed);\n\n    let result = svc.send(Event::SelectDate {\n        date: date(2024, 3, 15),\n    });\n\n    assert_eq!(*svc.state(), State::Closed);\n    assert!(!result.state_changed);\n    // The value still commits (and notifies) even though the popover stays put.\n    assert_eq!(svc.context().selected_date(), Some(date(2024, 3, 15)));\n    assert_eq!(effects(result), vec![Effect::ValueChange]);\n}\n\n#[test]\nfn vetoed_controlled_focus_open_then_close_clears_stale_focus_intent() {\n    // A controlled focus-in open records a deferred `OpenFocus::None` intent and\n    // requests the open via `OpenChange`. If the parent vetoes (leaves\n    // `open: Some(false)`), no `SyncProps` consumes the intent. A subsequent\n    // no-op close (`FocusOut` while already closed) must cancel the stale intent\n    // so a *later* programmatic open uses the per-direction default\n    // (`FocusCalendar`) rather than the stale \"don't move focus\" marker.\n    let mut svc = service_with(\n        Props {\n            open: Some(false),\n            ..props()\n        },\n        en_us(),\n    );\n\n    // Focus-in requests an open; parent vetoes, so state stays closed and a\n    // deferred `OpenFocus::None` intent lingers in `requested_focus`.\n    drop(svc.send(Event::FocusIn));\n    assert_eq!(*svc.state(), State::Closed);\n    assert_eq!(svc.context().requested_focus, Some(OpenFocus::None));\n\n    // The field loses focus before the parent ever honoured the open: a no-op\n    // close that must clear the stale intent.\n    drop(svc.send(Event::FocusOut));\n    assert_eq!(svc.context().requested_focus, None);\n\n    // The parent later opens programmatically: the calendar must receive focus.\n    let emitted = effects(svc.set_props(Props {\n        open: Some(true),\n        ..props()\n    }));\n    assert!(emitted.contains(&Effect::FocusCalendar));\n}\n\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n// Snapshots — root\n// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────"
}
```

Now fix #1: pass `ctx` to `open_request` and clear stale intent on a no-op close. Update the signature and the early-return:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01KPgUp69xGuBpdbRYihCwP5
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-c59961ed/crates/ars-components/src/date_time/date_picker/mod.rs",
  "old_string": "fn open_request(\n    state: &State,\n    props: &Props,\n    target_open: bool,\n    focus: OpenFocus,\n) -> Option<TransitionPlan<Machine>> {\n    if (*state == State::Open) == target_open {\n        return None;\n    }",
  "new_string": "fn open_request(\n    state: &State,\n    ctx: &Context,\n    props: &Props,\n    target_open: bool,\n    focus: OpenFocus,\n) -> Option<TransitionPlan<Machine>> {\n    if (*state == State::Open) == target_open {\n        // Already in the requested open-state, so there is nothing to\n        // reconcile. A controlled open the parent vetoed (left `open`\n        // unchanged) leaves a deferred focus intent in `requested_focus` with no\n        // `SyncProps` to consume it; a subsequent no-op *close* (e.g. `FocusOut`\n        // after the input lost focus) cancels that stale intent so a later\n        // programmatic open uses the per-direction default (`FocusCalendar`)\n        // instead of suppressing it. (`requested_focus` is only ever set under\n        // controlled `open`, so the uncontrolled no-op path stays a pure `None`.)\n        if !target_open && ctx.requested_focus.is_some() {\n            return Some(TransitionPlan::new().apply(|ctx: &mut Context| {\n                ctx.requested_focus = None;\n            }));\n        }\n        return None;\n    }"
}
```

1830	pub enum DateOrder {
1831	    MonthDayYear, // e.g. en-US — MM/dd/yyyy
1832	    DayMonthYear, // e.g. en-GB — dd/MM/yyyy (most common worldwide)
1833	    YearMonthDay, // e.g. ja-JP — yyyy/MM/dd

1835	
1836	/// Returns the locale's preferred numeric date field order. Resolves from real
1837	/// locale data when a backend is compiled in (CLDR via ICU4X, or the browser
1838	/// `Intl` API on wasm) and falls back to a `(language, region)` heuristic.
1839	pub fn date_order(locale: &Locale) -> DateOrder;
1840	```
1841	
1842	Normative behavior:
1843	
1844	- `DateFormatter::new(locale, length)` remains the compatibility constructor for simple date-only formatting. Existing callers continue to use the backend-native simple style path.
1845	- `new_with_options(...)` is the canonical public constructor for the richer formatter surface.
1846	- `format_*_to_parts(...)` returns a backend-stable normalized part model. ICU4X and browser `Intl` may tokenize internally in different ways, but the public `DateFormatterPartKind` contract is shared.
1847	- `format_*_range(...)` collapses identical leading and trailing segments into a single shared rendering and inserts a shared separator between differing middle segments.
1848	- `format_*_range_to_parts(...)` exposes the same collapsed rendering with explicit `Shared`, `Start`, and `End` range-part metadata.
1849	- `resolved_options()` reports the effective locale, calendar, numbering system, time zone, hour cycle, and style shortcuts after locale defaults and explicit overrides are applied.
1850	- The `calendar` option overrides the locale calendar for display purposes. Date-like inputs are projected into the resolved display calendar before field parts are produced.
1851	- The `hour_cycle` option overrides locale hour-cycle defaults when time formatting is in use.
1852	- `format_zoned(...)` and `format_zoned_to_parts(...)` are available only on `std` builds because `ZonedDateTime` is only available there.
1853	- `format_zoned_range(...)` and `format_zoned_range_to_parts(...)` are also `std`-only for the same reason.
1854	- `Short` and `Narrow` textual month and weekday widths are normalized backend-stable labels; callers must not rely on raw ICU4X and raw browser tokenization being byte-for-byte identical.
1855	- `TimeZoneNameFormat` exposes a stable time-zone label surface. Backends may normalize exact wording while preserving the chosen label category.
1856	- `queries::get_hours_in_day(date, time_zone)` measures the elapsed hours between the resolved start of `date` and the resolved start of the next calendar day in the supplied time zone, so DST transitions yield `23` or `25` instead of a fixed `24`.

```
653	                // Suppress a no-op `ValueChange` when the date is already selected.

671	                    // composed calendar props.

673	                    ctx.input_text = ctx.formatted_value();
```

```
815	}

817	/// Builds the plan for a user-driven request to open or close.
818	///
819	/// When `open` is **controlled** (`props.open.is_some()`), `State` is not mutated:
820	/// the request is recorded in `requested_open` (and the `focus` intent in
821	/// `requested_focus`) and signalled via `OpenChange`; the parent reconciles via
822	/// the prop and the focus effect fires from `SyncProps` once the state lands.
823	/// When **uncontrolled**, `State` transitions and the `focus` effect fires now.
824	/// Returns `None` when already in the requested open state.
825	fn open_request(
826	    state: &State,
827	    props: &Props,
828	    target_open: bool,
829	    focus: OpenFocus,
830	) -> Option<TransitionPlan<Machine>> {
831	    if (*state == State::Open) == target_open { return None; }
832	    let controlled = props.open.is_some();
833	    let mut plan = if controlled {
834	        TransitionPlan::new()
835	    } else {
836	        TransitionPlan::to(if target_open { State::Open } else { State::Closed })
837	    };
838	    plan = plan
839	        .apply(move |ctx| {
840	            ctx.requested_open = target_open;
841	            if controlled { ctx.requested_focus = Some(focus); }
842	        })
843	        .with_effect(PendingEffect::named(Effect::OpenChange));
844	    if !controlled && let Some(focus) = focus.effect() {
845	        plan = plan.with_effect(focus);
846	    }
847	    Some(plan)
848	}
849	
850	/// Re-applies the controlled value and cached scalar prop fields onto `ctx`.
851	/// Open/closed is owned by `State` (the `SyncProps` transition derives the
852	/// target state from `props.open`), so there is no `open` field to refresh.
853	///
854	/// The input text is re-synced **only when the displayed value or format
855	/// actually changed** — an unrelated prop change (e.g. `invalid`, `description`,
856	/// `disabled`) must not clobber a partial/invalid date the user is typing.
857	fn sync_props_into_ctx(ctx: &mut Context, props: &Props) {
858	    let previous_display = ctx.formatted_value();
859	    ctx.value.sync_controlled(props.value.clone());
860	    ctx.min = props.min.clone();
861	    ctx.max = props.max.clone();
862	    ctx.today = props.today.clone();
863	    ctx.disabled = props.disabled;
864	    ctx.readonly = props.readonly;
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-c59961ed/spec/components/date-time/date-picker.md",
  "old_string": "/// When **uncontrolled**, `State` transitions and the `focus` effect fires now.\n/// Returns `None` when already in the requested open state.\nfn open_request(\n    state: &State,\n    props: &Props,\n    target_open: bool,\n    focus: OpenFocus,\n) -> Option<TransitionPlan<Machine>> {\n    if (*state == State::Open) == target_open { return None; }",
  "new_string": "/// When **uncontrolled**, `State` transitions and the `focus` effect fires now.\n/// Returns `None` when already in the requested open state — except that a no-op\n/// *close* clears any stale `requested_focus` left by a controlled open the\n/// parent vetoed (so a later programmatic open uses the per-direction default\n/// rather than a stale \"don't move focus\" intent).\nfn open_request(\n    state: &State,\n    ctx: &Context,\n    props: &Props,\n    target_open: bool,\n    focus: OpenFocus,\n) -> Option<TransitionPlan<Machine>> {\n    if (*state == State::Open) == target_open {\n        // A controlled open the parent vetoed leaves a deferred focus intent\n        // with no `SyncProps` to consume it; a subsequent no-op close (e.g.\n        // `FocusOut` after the input lost focus) cancels it. `requested_focus`\n        // is only ever set under controlled `open`, so the uncontrolled no-op\n        // path stays a pure `None`.\n        if !target_open && ctx.requested_focus.is_some() {\n            return Some(TransitionPlan::new().apply(|ctx| { ctx.requested_focus = None; }));\n        }\n        return None;\n    }"
}
```

> AGENT

I've addressed all three Codex P2 findings on PR #697 with TDD (test-first, then fix), and synced the spec. Here's the summary before I commit:

## Codex review #697 — pass 7 (3 P2 findings)

**1. Stale controlled focus intents after vetoed opens** (`date_picker/mod.rs:752`)
A controlled focus-in open records `requested_focus = Some(OpenFocus::None)` and waits for `SyncProps`. If the parent vetoes (leaves `open: Some(false)`), no `SyncProps` consumes it, and a later programmatic open inherits the stale "don't move focus" marker, suppressing `FocusCalendar`.
- **Fix:** `open_request` now takes `ctx`; a no-op *close* (e.g. `FocusOut` after the input lost focus) clears any pending `requested_focus`. The uncontrolled path stays a pure `None` (intent is only ever set under controlled `open`).
- **Test:** `vetoed_controlled_focus_open_then_close_clears_stale_focus_intent`.

**2. ICU field order for native-digit / non-Gregorian locales** (`date.rs:424` → root cause in `ars-i18n`)
`date_order` formatted a Gregorian probe and read back the field order, but for `fa-IR` ICU (a) converts to the Solar Hijri calendar (`۱۳۸۵/۹/۱` — values no longer 11/22/2006) and (b) emits Persian digits the parser couldn't read, so it fell back to the heuristic (`DayMonthYear`) when ICU actually orders year-first.
- **Fix:** the probe now forces the Gregorian calendar (`Locale::with_gregorian_calendar()` via `u-ca-gregory`) so values stay recognizable, and `numeric_field_order_from_formatted` transliterates native digits via the existing `normalize_digits`. Verified empirically: `fa-IR`→`YearMonthDay`, `ar-EG`→`DayMonthYear`, en-US/ja-JP unchanged.
- **Test:** `date_order_uses_icu_order_for_native_digit_and_non_gregorian_locales`.

**3. Closed pickers stay closed on stale selections** (`date_picker/mod.rs:1031`)
With `close_on_select = false`, a `SelectDate` reaching an already-closed uncontrolled picker forced `State::Open`, reopening the popover after an outside-close/focus-out race.
- **Fix:** the "stay open" branch now preserves the current state (`state.clone()`) instead of forcing `Open`. Value still commits + notifies.
- **Test:** `select_date_on_closed_uncontrolled_picker_stays_closed`.

**Spec sync:** `spec/components/date-time/date-picker.md` updated to match (open_request signature/early-return, SelectDate `next_state`). Finding #2 needed no spec change — foundation 04 already promises real-data order; the fix removes the drift.

**Verification:** ars-i18n lib 318 passed; ars-components date_time 446 passed + 148 date_picker; spec-conformance 3 passed; date_picker proptest passed (512 cases); clippy clean on both crates.

Per your standing preference I won't commit/push without approval. Shall I run `cargo xci-fast`, then commit and push to PR #697 (and continue the Codex loop: reply inline, resolve the three threads, re-trigger `@codex review`)?

Covered by \\`vetoed_controlled_focus_open_then_close_clears_stale_focus_intent\\`; spec synced.\" >/dev/null && echo \"replied 1\"\n\ngh api -X POST repos/fogodev/ars-ui/pulls/697/comments \\\n  -F in_reply_to=3330370979 \\\n  -f body=\"Fixed in ${SHA}: the \\`date_order\\` probe now forces the Gregorian calendar via \\`Locale::with_gregorian_calendar\\` (so non-Gregorian-default locales like fa-IR keep recognisable year/month/day probe values instead of converting to Solar Hijri), and \\`numeric_field_order_from_formatted\\` transliterates native digits via the existing \\`normalize_digits\\`. Verified empirically: fa-IR resolves to \\`YearMonthDay\\`, ar-EG to \\`DayMonthYear\\`, en-US/ja-JP unchanged. Covered by \\`date_order_uses_icu_order_for_native_digit_and_non_gregorian_locales\\`.\" >/dev/null && echo \"replied 2\"\n\ngh api -X POST repos/fogodev/ars-ui/pulls/697/comments \\\n  -F in_reply_to=3330370980 \\\n  -f body=\"Fixed in ${SHA}: the uncontrolled \\\"stay open\\\" branch now preserves the current state (\\`state.clone()\\`) instead of forcing \\`State::Open\\`, so a queued/stale \\`SelectDate\\` that races with an outside-close/focus-out can no longer reopen an already-closed picker. The value still commits and \\`ValueChange\\` fires. Covered by \\`select_date_on_closed_uncontrolled_picker_stays_closed\\`; spec synced.\" >/dev/null && echo \"replied 3\"",
```json
  "description": "Reply inline on all three threads"
}
```

```json
  "description": "Poll Codex reactions/threads until verdict",
  "timeout": 600000,
  "run_in_background": true
}
```

```
        run: cargo xtask ci coverage
      # Reuses the merged lcov.info from the coverage step (no recompile).
      # Regression-mode gate: fails the job only when a tracked (>= CRAP 30)
      # shipped-library function regresses vs the committed baseline. Output is
      # the human table in the job log (no GitHub annotations).
      - name: CRAP gate
        run: cargo xtask ci crap
      - name: Upload coverage diagnostics
        if: failure()
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v7
        with:
          name: coverage-diagnostics
          path: |
            lcov.info
            target/coverage/*.lcov
            target/wasm-coverage/**/profraw/*.profraw
          if-no-files-found: ignore
      - name: Snapshot Count
        run: cargo xtask ci snapshot-count
      # Always publish the merged lcov so the CRAP baseline can be regenerated
      # with `cargo xcrap --update-baseline` from a real native+wasm run.
      - name: Upload merged lcov
        if: always()
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v7
        with:
          name: coverage-lcov
          path: lcov.info
          if-no-files-found: warn
      - name: Upload to Codecov
        if: always()
        uses: codecov/codecov-action@v5
        with:
          files: lcov.info
          fail_ci_if_error: true
          token: ${{ secrets.CODECOV_TOKEN }}

  feature-flags-core:
    name: Feature Flags — Core
    needs: [clippy]
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
      - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable
      - uses: Swatinem/rust-cache@v2
        with:
          shared-key: feature-flags
      - uses: taiki-e/install-action@nextest
      - name: Feature Flags — Core
        run: cargo xtask ci feature-matrix-core

  feature-flags-i18n:
=== xcrap alias ===
7:xcrap    = "xtask crap"
```

Found the root cause. The CRAP verdict: **`↑ 1 regressed`** — `fallback_date_order` (ars-i18n/src/date.rs) jumped from CRAP 5.0 → 30.0 (+25.0) because it dropped to **0% coverage**. My fix made `icu_date_order` succeed for native-digit locales, so the fallback path that those probes used to exercise is no longer hit. The fix: add a direct unit test for `fallback_date_order`, restoring its coverage (CRAP back to ~5, matching baseline).

```
=== fallback_date_order body ===
fn fallback_date_order(locale: &Locale) -> DateOrder {
    if matches!(locale.language(), "ja" | "zh" | "ko") {
        return DateOrder::YearMonthDay;
    }

    match locale.region() {
        Some("US" | "FM" | "PW") => DateOrder::MonthDayYear,

        Some("CA" | "CN" | "HU" | "JP" | "KR" | "LT" | "MN" | "TW") => DateOrder::YearMonthDay,

        _ => DateOrder::DayMonthYear,
    }
}

#[cfg(any(
    feature = "icu4x",
    all(feature = "web-intl", target_arch = "wasm32", not(feature = "icu4x"))
))]
```

```json
  "new_string": "        // Arabic-Egypt uses Arabic-Indic digits in a day/month/year order; the\n        // transliteration must let ICU's real order win over the heuristic.\n        assert_eq!(\n            date_order(&Locale::parse(\"ar-EG\").expect(\"locale should parse\")),\n            DateOrder::DayMonthYear,\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn fallback_date_order_covers_language_and_region_branches() {\n        // The `(language, region)` heuristic is the no-backend fallback (and the\n        // path `date_order` takes only when the ICU/JS probe yields nothing).\n        // Exercise every branch directly so it stays covered regardless of which\n        // locales happen to reach it through the backend probe.\n        use super::fallback_date_order;\n\n        let order = |tag: &str| fallback_date_order(&Locale::parse(tag).expect(\"locale parses\"));\n\n        // CJK languages are year-first regardless of region.\n        assert_eq!(order(\"ja-JP\"), DateOrder::YearMonthDay);\n        assert_eq!(order(\"zh-CN\"), DateOrder::YearMonthDay);\n        assert_eq!(order(\"ko-KR\"), DateOrder::YearMonthDay);\n\n        // Month-first regions.\n        assert_eq!(order(\"en-US\"), DateOrder::MonthDayYear);\n\n        // Year-first regions selected by region rather than language.\n        assert_eq!(order(\"en-CA\"), DateOrder::YearMonthDay);\n\n        // Everything else is day-first (the most common order worldwide).\n        assert_eq!(order(\"en-GB\"), DateOrder::DayMonthYear);\n        assert_eq!(order(\"de-DE\"), DateOrder::DayMonthYear);\n    }\n"
}
```

```
=== PRRT_kwDORp4enM6F86xc ===
PATH: crates/ars-components/src/date_time/date_picker/mod.rs:1080
DBID: 3330497197

**<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Gate close effects on an actual open state**

When a queued/stale `SelectDate` arrives after an uncontrolled picker has already closed (for example a calendar click racing with `FocusOut`), `should_close` is still true even though `state` is `Closed`, so this emits `OpenChange` and `RestoreFocusToInput` without any open-state transition. That can call `on_open_change(false)` spuriously and pull focus back into the input after focus has left; only add these close effects when the selection actually closes an open picker.

Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.

=== PRRT_kwDORp4enM6F86xe ===
PATH: crates/ars-components/src/date_time/date_picker/mod.rs:145
DBID: 3330497199

**<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Add a value-change callback to Props**

`ValueChange` is emitted for typed and calendar selections, but unlike `OpenChange` there is no `Props::on_value_change` for the adapter to call. In controlled `value` mode this leaves the parent with no public callback channel to learn `Context::requested_value`, so user selections and clears can be recorded internally without any way for consumers to echo the new value back.

Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.

=== PRRT_kwDORp4enM6F86xg ===
PATH: crates/ars-components/src/date_time/date_picker/mod.rs:548
DBID: 3330497201

**<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Normalize localized digits before parsing**

For locales where users enter native digits (for example Arabic-Indic or Extended Arabic-Indic numerals), this ASCII-only check classifies an otherwise valid date as partial before `parse_date` can commit it. With an existing value, typing a localized numeric date leaves the old selected date and hidden input in place even though the visible field changed; normalize digits with the existing i18n helper before the completeness and parse checks.

Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.

=== PRRT_kwDORp4enM6F86xi ===
PATH: crates/ars-components/src/date_time/date_picker/mod.rs:988
DBID: 3330497204

**<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Let readonly pickers open for viewing**

When `readonly` is true, this guard blocks the trigger and ArrowDown open paths, and the focus path is also gated elsewhere, so a read-only picker cannot open the calendar at all. That contradicts the prop contract that read-only allows viewing but not editing; keep the input/selection mutation guards, but allow opening so users can inspect the current date/month without changing the value.

Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.
```

```
                let next_state = if !open_controlled && should_close {

                let mut plan = TransitionPlan::to(next_state).apply(move |ctx: &mut Context| {
                    // Record the requested date so the adapter can forward it
                    // even when `value` is controlled (where `get()` returns the

                    ctx.requested_value = Some(date.clone());

                    // Derive the visible text and `parsed_date` from the value

                    ctx.parsed_date = ctx.value.get().clone();
                    ctx.input_text = ctx.formatted_value();
```

```
    CalendarDate::new_gregorian(year, month, day).ok()

/// Classification of input text for [`Event::InputChange`].
enum InputClass {
    /// A complete, valid date.
    Valid(CalendarDate),
    /// A complete numeric entry (three numeric fields) that does not form a
    /// valid date, e.g. `02/30/2024`. Treated as a rejected commit (clears the
    /// value) rather than as in-progress typing.
    CompleteInvalid,
    /// In-progress / non-numeric text the user is still editing.
    Partial,
}

/// Classifies input text as a valid date, a complete-but-invalid date, or
/// in-progress partial text.
///
/// A "complete" entry is exactly three numeric fields in the format's separator;
/// only such entries can be rejected as invalid. Anything else (too few fields,
/// non-numeric) is partial and leaves the committed value untouched.
fn classify_input(text: &str, format: &str) -> InputClass {
    let (separator, _order) = parse_format(format);
    let tokens = text.split(separator).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let complete_numeric = tokens.len() == 3
        && tokens.iter().all(|token| {
            let token = token.trim();
            !token.is_empty() && token.bytes().all(|byte| byte.is_ascii_digit())
        });

    if !complete_numeric {
        return InputClass::Partial;
    }

    match parse_date(text, format) {
        Some(date) => InputClass::Valid(date),
        None => InputClass::CompleteInvalid,
    }
}

// Messages
// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/// `MessageFn` carrying a formatted-date plus locale label closure.
pub type SelectedDateLabelFn = dyn Fn(&str, &Locale) -> String + Send + Sync;

/// Locale-specific labels for the `DatePicker` component.
///
/// Calendar navigation labels (prev/next month, today, unavailable) belong to
/// the embedded [`Calendar`](super::calendar)'s messages and are not duplicated
/// here.
```

```
491	/// Parses a date string according to the given pattern.
492	///
493	/// Returns `None` when the text does not split into three numeric tokens in the
494	/// pattern's field order or does not form a valid Gregorian date.
495	fn parse_date(text: &str, format: &str) -> Option<CalendarDate> {
496	    let (sep, order) = parse_format(format);
497	
498	    let tokens = text.split(sep).collect::<Vec<_>>();
499	
500	    if tokens.len() != 3 {
501	        return None;
502	    }
503	
504	    let (mut year, mut month, mut day) = (None, None, None);
505	
506	    for (index, field) in order.iter().enumerate() {
507	        let parsed: i64 = tokens[index].trim().parse().ok()?;
508	
509	        match field {
510	            DateField::Year => year = Some(parsed),
511	            DateField::Month => month = Some(parsed),
512	            DateField::Day => day = Some(parsed),
513	        }
514	    }
515	
516	    let year = i32::try_from(year?).ok()?;
517	    let month = u8::try_from(month?).ok()?;
518	    let day = u8::try_from(day?).ok()?;
519	
520	    CalendarDate::new_gregorian(year, month, day).ok()
521	}
```

```json
  "old_string": "fn classify_input(text: &str, format: &str) -> InputClass {\n    let (separator, _order) = parse_format(format);\n    let tokens = text.split(separator).collect::<Vec<_>>();\n    let complete_numeric = tokens.len() == 3\n        && tokens.iter().all(|token| {\n            let token = token.trim();\n            !token.is_empty() && token.bytes().all(|byte| byte.is_ascii_digit())\n        });\n\n    if !complete_numeric {\n        return InputClass::Partial;\n    }\n\n    match parse_date(text, format) {\n        Some(date) => InputClass::Valid(date),\n        None => InputClass::CompleteInvalid,\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "fn classify_input(text: &str, format: &str) -> InputClass {\n    // Transliterate native decimal digits to ASCII before the completeness\n    // check so a localized numeric date (e.g. Arabic-Indic) is recognised as a\n    // complete entry that can commit, not misclassified as in-progress text.\n    let text = normalize_digits(text);\n    let (separator, _order) = parse_format(format);\n    let tokens = text.split(separator).collect::<Vec<_>>();\n    let complete_numeric = tokens.len() == 3\n        && tokens.iter().all(|token| {\n            let token = token.trim();\n            !token.is_empty() && token.bytes().all(|byte| byte.is_ascii_digit())\n        });\n\n    if !complete_numeric {\n        return InputClass::Partial;\n    }\n\n    match parse_date(&text, format) {\n        Some(date) => InputClass::Valid(date),\n        None => InputClass::CompleteInvalid,\n    }\n}"
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-c59961ed/crates/ars-components/src/date_time/date_picker/tests.rs",
  "old_string": "    // The popover stays closed — the selection must not reopen it.\n    assert_eq!(*svc.state(), State::Closed);\n    // The value still commits (and notifies) even though the popover stays put.\n    assert_eq!(svc.context().value.get().as_ref(), Some(&date(2024, 3, 15)));\n    assert_eq!(effects(result), vec![Effect::ValueChange]);\n}",
  "new_string": "    // The popover stays closed — the selection must not reopen it.\n    assert_eq!(*svc.state(), State::Closed);\n    // The value still commits (and notifies) even though the popover stays put.\n    assert_eq!(svc.context().value.get().as_ref(), Some(&date(2024, 3, 15)));\n    assert_eq!(effects(result), vec![Effect::ValueChange]);\n}\n\n#[test]\nfn select_date_on_closed_picker_emits_no_close_effects() {\n    // With the default `close_on_select == true`, a stale/queued `SelectDate`\n    // that lands after the picker already closed (e.g. a calendar click racing\n    // with `FocusOut`) must NOT emit `OpenChange`/`RestoreFocusToInput` for a\n    // close that never happens — those would fire `on_open_change(false)`\n    // spuriously and pull focus back into the input. Only the value commits.\n    let mut svc = service();\n    assert_eq!(*svc.state(), State::Closed);\n\n    let result = svc.send(Event::SelectDate {\n        date: date(2024, 3, 15),\n    });\n\n    assert_eq!(*svc.state(), State::Closed);\n    let emitted = effects(result);\n    assert!(emitted.contains(&Effect::ValueChange));\n    assert!(!emitted.contains(&Effect::OpenChange));\n    assert!(!emitted.contains(&Effect::RestoreFocusToInput));\n}\n\n#[test]\nfn input_change_commits_native_digit_date() {\n    // A user typing a localized numeric date (Extended Arabic-Indic digits for\n    // `fa-IR`) must commit just like ASCII digits — the input classifier and\n    // parser transliterate native digits before the completeness/parse checks,\n    // so the value is not left stale as \"in-progress\" text.\n    let mut svc = service_with(props(), en_us());\n\n    // ۰۳/۱۵/۲۰۲۴ == 03/15/2024 in Extended Arabic-Indic digits.\n    let result = svc.send(Event::InputChange {\n        value: String::from(\"\\u{06F0}\\u{06F3}/\\u{06F1}\\u{06F5}/\\u{06F2}\\u{06F0}\\u{06F2}\\u{06F4}\"),\n    });\n\n    assert_eq!(svc.context().value.get().as_ref(), Some(&date(2024, 3, 15)));\n    assert!(effects(result).contains(&Effect::ValueChange));\n}"
}
```

> AGENT

The calendar sibling confirms the pattern: a readonly calendar still renders/opens but blocks *selection* (`if ctx.readonly { return None }` only on mutation). So readonly = "view, don't edit." This supports Codex — but the date-picker spec explicitly contradicts itself (Props doc says "viewing," state machine blocks opening with deliberate comments + tests). Since this reverses deliberate, tested behavior, I'll confirm the direction with you:

> TOOL

But the date-picker state machine deliberately blocks opening (Open/ArrowDown/FocusIn guards) with passing tests enforcing it. How should I resolve this?",
```json
      "header": "readonly open",
      "multiSelect": false,
      "options": [
        {
          "label": "Allow opening, block editing (Recommended)",

Update the spec + flip the readonly_blocks_open tests. Matches the Props doc, the Calendar sibling, and Codex."
        },
        {
          "label": "Keep blocking open",

}
```

245	/// Parse a date string in the pattern's field order. `new_gregorian` is fallible
246	/// (validates the date), so it is mapped to `Option` via `.ok()`.
247	fn parse_date(text: &str, format: &str) -> Option<CalendarDate> {
248	    let (sep, order) = parse_format(format);
249	    let tokens: Vec<&str> = text.split(sep).collect();
250	    if tokens.len() != 3 { return None; }
251	    let (mut year, mut month, mut day) = (None, None, None);
252	    for (index, field) in order.iter().enumerate() {
253	        let parsed: i64 = tokens[index].trim().parse().ok()?;
254	        match field {
255	            DateField::Year => year = Some(parsed),
256	            DateField::Month => month = Some(parsed),
257	            DateField::Day => day = Some(parsed),
258	        }
259	    }
260	    let year = i32::try_from(year?).ok()?;
261	    let month = u8::try_from(month?).ok()?;
262	    let day = u8::try_from(day?).ok()?;
263	    CalendarDate::new_gregorian(year, month, day).ok()
264	}
265	
266	/// Classification of input text for `Event::InputChange`.
267	enum InputClass {
268	    /// A complete, valid date.
269	    Valid(CalendarDate),
270	    /// Three numeric fields that do not form a valid date (e.g. `02/30/2024`) —
271	    /// treated as a rejected commit, not in-progress text.
272	    CompleteInvalid,
273	    /// In-progress / non-numeric text still being edited.
274	    Partial,
275	}
276	
277	/// A "complete" entry is exactly three numeric fields in the format separator;
278	/// only those can be rejected as invalid. Anything else is `Partial`.
279	fn classify_input(text: &str, format: &str) -> InputClass {
280	    let (sep, _order) = parse_format(format);
281	    let tokens: Vec<&str> = text.split(sep).collect();
282	    let complete_numeric = tokens.len() == 3
283	        && tokens.iter().all(|t| {
284	            let t = t.trim();
285	            !t.is_empty() && t.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_digit())
286	        });
287	    if !complete_numeric { return InputClass::Partial; }
288	    match parse_date(text, format) {
289	        Some(date) => InputClass::Valid(date),
290	        None => InputClass::CompleteInvalid,
291	    }
292	}

Native decimal\n/// digits (e.g. Arabic-Indic) are transliterated to ASCII first so a localized\n/// numeric date still commits.\nfn parse_date(text: &str, format: &str) -> Option<CalendarDate> {\n    let text = normalize_digits(text);\n    let (sep, order) = parse_format(format);\n    let tokens: Vec<&str> = text.split(sep).collect();"

```json
{
```

```json
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-c59961ed/spec/components/date-time/date-picker.md",
  "old_string": "fn classify_input(text: &str, format: &str) -> InputClass {\n    let (sep, _order) = parse_format(format);\n    let tokens: Vec<&str> = text.split(sep).collect();\n    let complete_numeric = tokens.len() == 3\n        && tokens.iter().all(|t| {\n            let t = t.trim();\n            !t.is_empty() && t.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_digit())\n        });\n    if !complete_numeric { return InputClass::Partial; }\n    match parse_date(text, format) {\n        Some(date) => InputClass::Valid(date),\n        None => InputClass::CompleteInvalid,\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "fn classify_input(text: &str, format: &str) -> InputClass {\n    // Transliterate native decimal digits to ASCII before the completeness check\n    // so a localized numeric date is recognised as complete (and can commit),\n    // not misclassified as in-progress text.\n    let text = normalize_digits(text);\n    let (sep, _order) = parse_format(format);\n    let tokens: Vec<&str> = text.split(sep).collect();\n    let complete_numeric = tokens.len() == 3\n        && tokens.iter().all(|t| {\n            let t = t.trim();\n            !t.is_empty() && t.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_digit())\n        });\n    if !complete_numeric { return InputClass::Partial; }\n    match parse_date(&text, format) {\n        Some(date) => InputClass::Valid(date),\n        None => InputClass::CompleteInvalid,\n    }\n}"
}
```

```
1048	
1049	    /// Get the trigger attributes.
1050	    pub fn trigger_attrs(&self) -> AttrMap {
1051	        let mut attrs = AttrMap::new();
1052	        let [(scope_attr, scope_val), (part_attr, part_val)] = Part::Trigger.data_attrs();
1053	        attrs.set(scope_attr, scope_val);
1054	        attrs.set(part_attr, part_val);
1055	        attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Id, self.ctx.ids.part("trigger"));
1056	        // Explicit `type="button"` so activating the trigger never submits a
1057	        // surrounding form (the HTML default button type is `submit`).
1058	        attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Type, "button");
1059	        attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::Label), (self.ctx.messages.trigger_label)(&self.ctx.locale));
1060	        // The trigger advertises the popup it opens (matches the Popover trigger
1061	        // convention); the Input carries the same `aria-haspopup`.
1062	        attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::HasPopup), "dialog");
1063	        attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::Expanded), if self.is_open() { "true" } else { "false" });
1064	        attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::Controls), self.ctx.ids.part("content"));
1065	        attrs.set(HtmlAttr::TabIndex, "0");
1066	        // `readonly` blocks every opening path, so the trigger advertises it is
1067	        // non-actionable rather than rendering enabled-but-inert (matches the
1068	        // input and clear-trigger, which already expose readonly/disabled).
1069	        if self.ctx.disabled || self.ctx.readonly {
1070	            attrs.set_bool(HtmlAttr::Disabled, true);
1071	            attrs.set(HtmlAttr::Aria(AriaAttr::Disabled), "true");
1072	        }
1073	        attrs
1074	    }
```

```
1	//! `spec compile-snippets` — parse Rust code blocks in spec markdown
2	//! through `syn::parse_file` and surface syntax errors.
3	//!
4	//! ## What this catches
5	//!
6	//! - Top-level Rust syntax bugs in spec snippets: missing braces,
7	//!   mistyped `pub fn`, unterminated string literals, unbalanced
8	//!   generics outside macro bodies, etc.
9	//! - Coverage across the full spec corpus —
10	//!   `spec/{components,foundation,leptos-components,dioxus-components,shared,testing}/`.
11	//!   The pre-existing `lint-code` linter only walked
12	//!   `spec/components/` and never invoked a Rust parser, so foundation
13	//!   and per-adapter specs had zero parse coverage.
14	//!
15	//! ## What this **does not** catch (important — read this)
16	//!
17	//! - **Macro-internal syntax.** `view! { … }` and `rsx! { … }` are
18	//!   opaque token trees to both `syn` and `rustc`; everything inside
19	//!   the outer braces is unchecked. The original Leptos `view!`
20	//!   regression (an inline `|errors: ArcRwSignal<Errors>|` closure
21	//!   where the macro parser misread `<Errors>` as an HTML tag) sits
22	//!   *inside* the macro and would not be caught here. The unit test
23	//!   suites at `crates/ars-{leptos,dioxus}/tests/` are the source of
24	//!   truth for macro-internal correctness.
25	//! - **Type / path resolution.** A snippet that names a type defined
26	//!   nowhere in the workspace still parses syntactically. Use
27	//!   `cargo check` against a fixture crate for that.
28	//! - **Borrow-checker / ownership errors.** Same as above —
29	//!   `syn::parse_file` is purely a parser.
30	//!
31	//! ## Opting out
32	//!
33	//! Code blocks that intentionally contain partial or pseudo-Rust
34	//! (signatures shown for documentation purposes, top-level expressions
35	//! meant to live inside an outer fn body, etc.) opt out by tagging the
36	//! fence info-string with `rust,no_check` (also recognised:
37	//! `rust,ignore`, `rust,no_run`, mirroring rustdoc). The extractor
38	//! honors those markers and skips the block.
39	//!
40	//! ## Performance
41	//!
42	//! Fast: no compiler invocation. Runs in milliseconds across all 2000+
43	//! Rust blocks in the spec, so it is suitable for inclusion in the
44	//! `cargo xci` pipeline (see `xtask::ci::Step::SpecCompileSnippets`).
45	
```

```
729:    let coverage_dir = Path::new("target").join("coverage");

788:fn run_coverage_native() -> Result<(), Error> {
795:    let coverage_dir = Path::new("target").join("coverage");
797:    fs::create_dir_all(&coverage_dir).map_err(Error::Io)?;
fn run_coverage_native() -> Result<(), Error> {
    preflight_llvm_cov()?;

    preflight_nextest()?;

    coverage::preflight_nightly().map_err(Error::Coverage)?;

    let coverage_dir = Path::new("target").join("coverage");

    fs::create_dir_all(&coverage_dir).map_err(Error::Io)?;

    let lcov = coverage_dir.join("native-fast.lcov");

    // Scope the instrumented run to the crates whose CI coverage is native-only
    // (`coverage::native_thresholds`), so the local run is both fast and exactly
    // faithful to what CI enforces for them.
    let packages = coverage::native_coverage_packages();

    let mut args = vec![
        "+nightly",
        "llvm-cov",
        "nextest",
        "--branch",
        "--no-fail-fast",
    ];

    for package in &packages {
        args.push("-p");
        args.push(package);
    }

    args.push("--lcov");
    args.push("--output-path");
    args.push(lcov.to_str().expect("valid utf-8 path"));

    cargo(Step::CoverageNative, &args)?;

    match coverage::check_native(&lcov) {
        Ok(output) => {
            eprint!("{output}");
            Ok(())
        }

        Err(error) => Err(Error::Coverage(error)),
    }
}
```

CI is re-running on the new commit (the Coverage/CRAP job takes ~23 min). Let me record the coverage-gate gotcha I learned, since it'll bite future local verification:

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_01V3HrmfR2zry2n3iSxrqYZd
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.claude/projects/-Users-ericson-Workspace-Rust-ars-ui/memory/project_crap_and_native_coverage_gates.md",
  "content": "---\nname: project_crap_and_native_coverage_gates\ndescription: How the CRAP gate and native-only coverage work in CI, and the ars-i18n local-verification gotcha\nmetadata:\n  type: project\n---\n\nCI coverage gates (added to `main` ~2026-05, after the date_picker PR #697 branch was cut):\n\n- **CRAP gate** runs `cargo xtask ci crap` (impl in `xtask/src/crap.rs`, baseline `.crap-baseline.json`, `cargo-crap` 0.2.2). Regression-mode: fails only when a tracked (CRAP >= 30) shipped-library function gets *worse* than the committed baseline. It reuses the **full merged native+wasm `lcov.info`** the Coverage job produces, so it is **NOT in `cargo xci-fast`** — only in full `cargo xci` (needs clang-22 + wasm toolchain). Verdict line: `↑ N regressed ↓ improved ★ new ↔ moved · unchanged — removed`.\n- **`cargo xci-fast` now includes `coverage-native`** (`xtask/src/ci/mod.rs::run_coverage_native`), writing `target/coverage/native-fast.lcov`, scoped to `coverage::native_coverage_packages()` and enforcing per-crate thresholds.\n\n**Gotcha: `ars-i18n` is NOT in the native-only coverage set** (it has `web-intl` wasm paths), so `cargo xcov` / `xci-fast` coverage-native does **not** measure it. Its coverage/CRAP is only validated in the full wasm-merged CI lcov. To verify an `ars-i18n` function's coverage/CRAP locally: `cargo +nightly llvm-cov nextest --branch -p ars-i18n --lcov --output-path /tmp/i18n.lcov` then `cargo crap --path . --lcov /tmp/i18n.lcov --baseline .crap-baseline.json --min 0 [CI excludes] | grep <fn>` (ignore the false 0%/NEW rows for every non-i18n crate — a scoped lcov makes cargo-crap treat absent source as 0%).\n\n**Lesson (PR #697):** fixing a fallback so the primary path now succeeds can *drop* the fallback's incidental test coverage → CRAP regression (e.g. `fallback_date_order` 5→30 at 0%). Add a **direct unit test** for the fallback to restore coverage. See [[feedback_fix_all_review_findings]].\n"
}
```
